[D1.10]  CHECKER resolves=True  severity=clean
  LOCATION: m:150 mechanism: one permutation, one draw per slot, skip, k clip (totality and nesting)
  DOCUMENT SAYS:
    m:150 "One `permutation` of the eligible positions; walk in order; **each slot consumes exactly
    one replacement draw, accepted or skipped; no draw depends on k.**" and "k =
    clip(floor(0.15·n_eligible + 0.5), 3, floor(n_eligible/2)), half-up"
  RECORD SAYS:
    Computed at rates .10, .12, .15, .20, .25, .30 for every item. The accepted set at each rate is
    a subset of the next rate's, with identical letters (nested: True). k ties across rates through
    the clip, e.g. .10 and .12 both give sum 50 with all-3s except item 7. Min n_eligible is 6, so
    floor(n/2) ≥ 3 and the clip is never inverted; no 0.15n+0.5 lands on a float half-boundary.
  CHECKER NOTE:
    The mechanism is total and deterministic on the pool, and the nesting that §2.3's rationale
    relies on holds.
  REFUTER [lenient] resolves=True overturned=False severity=clean
    EVIDENCE:
      At rates .10/.12/.15/.20/.25/.30, k sums are 50/50/54/62/73/83. Every item reaches k at every
      rate, and each lower rate's accepted list is a prefix of the next rate's (nested: True). Float
      check: n=10 (items 6, 9, 14) does land on an exact half-boundary, .15·10+.5 = 2.0, which float
      resolves correctly as half-up (floor 2); the clip then lifts it to 3.
    REASONING:
      The mechanism is deterministic and total on the pool, and the nesting holds. The checker's
      sub-note that 'no 0.15n+0.5 lands on a float half-boundary' is literally false at n=10. It is
      harmless, because the float value is exact and the clip governs, so neither the verdict nor
      the severity changes.
  REFUTER [strict] resolves=True overturned=False severity=clean
    EVIDENCE:
      I tested rates .05 through .50 (11 rungs). The accepted sets are nested with identical letters
      at every rung. k sums are 48, 49, 50, 50, 54, 55, 62, 73, 83, 111, 130. The minimum n is 6, so
      floor(n/2) ≥ 3 and the clip is never inverted.
    REASONING:
      The mechanism is total and deterministic, and nesting holds. One correction to the checker's
      note: n=10 does land on an exact half (0.15·10 = 1.5). Float gives exactly 2.0 → floor 2,
      which matches half-up, and the clip makes k 3 anyway. No consequence.

  DOCUMENT SAYS:

  RECORD SAYS:
    Computed exactly: n₉₀ = smallest N with 10·(n^N − Σ_{x<floor} C(N,x)·k^x·(n−k)^(N−x)) ≥ 9·n^N,

    (scratchpad/agents/S1/launch2.py). An independent exact-Fraction linear-scan implementation
    (launch.py) agreed on every Q-F0 row. ⌈1.15·floor⌉: 96→111→128; 80→92→96; 32→37→64; 144→166→176;
    56→65→80. Floors and units are those of m:154–160. Every boundary is exact, and some are
    knife-edge. Cached A0, k=19: P(Bin(128,19/24)≥96)=.895926, under .90, so 160. Cached A0, k=13:
    P(Bin(192,13/24)≥96)=.890726, so 224. A2, k=24: P(Bin(256,24/40)≥144)=.900790, so 256. A2, k=34:
    P(Bin(176,34/40)≥144)=.898685, so 192. Uncached A0, k=21: n₉₀=96 exactly (P=.913171). A1s, k=23:
    n₉₀=64 exactly (P=.909326). Uncached A1, k=27: n₉₀=128 exactly (P=.902424).
  CHECKER NOTE:

    within .01 of .90, so Code's reproduction must use exact or high-precision arithmetic. A float
    tail can flip the k=19/20 cached row and the A2 k=24 and k=34 rows.

[X1.20]  CHECKER resolves=True  severity=minor
  LOCATION: m:146 (§2.1 gate-stage schedule rule) vs m:256 (G-F0m-Qwen), m:275 (G-F0m-OLMo)
  DOCUMENT SAYS:
    m:146 "the j-th session of the template (0-based) takes start order j mod 2 and, where its TOLD
    line names labels, clause order ⌊j/2⌋ mod 2 — so 16 sessions give 2 per cell, 8 give 1 per cell,
    4 cover 4 cells, and start and clause order are balanced across any m that is a multiple of 4";
    m:256 "first-pick share per pair for the sham template"
  RECORD SAYS:
    Computed (agents/X1/sched.py, all 8! permutations). The stated properties hold: m=16 gives 8/8
    start, 8/8 clause and 4/4/4/4 joint; m=8 gives 4/4 and 2/2/2/2; m=4 gives 2/2 and 1/1/1/1. But
    sessions j and j+8 share cell perm[j] and get identical start (j mod 2) and clause (⌊j/2⌋ mod
    2), so at m=16 the two sessions of each cell are duplicates in (pair, map, start, clause).
    Per-pair start balance holds in 22.9% of permutations, start and clause together in 3.8%, and on

  MISSED (refuter lenient) severity=minor
    LOCATION: m:283 §4 conjunct (ii); m:299 §5 orders (PASS/REVERSE and NULL-x); m:162, m:221
    DOCUMENT SAYS:
      m:283 "(ii) non-strict: each map's clean share ≥ .5 (≤ .5 for REVERSE)"; m:299 "NULL-x if (i)
      and (iii) hold in one direction and (ii) fails in that direction"
    RECORD SAYS:
      m:221 "an arm left below floor is analyzed as obtained"; m:162 "an arm that stops at its floor
      mid-unit is analyzed as launched, with per-map n reported". Computed: an A3 arm stopped by cap
      or PI at n = 5 domain sessions can satisfy (iii) (one-sided p = 1/32 = .03125 < .05). The
      first 5 cells of a balance unit's permutation(16) all share one map with probability
      2·C(8,5)/C(16,5) = 112/4368 = .0256.
    NOTE:
      No convention is given for (ii) when a map has no domain session, so POSITIVE versus NULL-x
      (and PASS versus INDETERMINATE for controls) is undefined in that reachable corner. The
      batteries, which 'reach every per-arm outcome' with skipped assertions failing, need a pinned
      convention. The gap predates v1.9 for (ii), but the new NULL-x predicate inherits it (ledger
      41).

  MISSED (refuter strict) severity=minor
    LOCATION: m:166 (§2.4 launch rule), m:62 (§0.10), m:84 (§0.11 STOP-5 A1 row)
    DOCUMENT SAYS:
      m:166 "Planning values by k (exact binomial; [S]; total over r — rows below the last listed k
      follow the same rule and are G-PWR's …)"; m:84 "→ §2.4 tables, §0.4 (**bounded per k; no
      open-ended total**)"
    RECORD SAYS:
      Computed: at k = 0 (r = 0), P(Bin(N, 0) ≥ floor) = 0 for every N, so n₉₀(0) does not exist and
      L is undefined. The tables stop at k = 1 (3,584 / 3,168 / 24,000 / 23,328). In Q-F0 and on
      OLMo, k = 0 can arise from pre-pick exclusions (e.g. lexical) without any bar firing, since
      lexical has no bar there (m:303).
    NOTE:
      The rule is not total over r as claimed. G-PWR cannot produce launch counts at k = 0. In
      practice the PI would decline v1.0 and the unit would be NOT-RUN, but the document should say
      so.
